        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-026",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-031",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 4.5's `_row_matches_topic` helper falls back to global behavior when (table, id) is not in self.artifact_topics \u2014 explicitly for backward compatibility. this is sensible but creates a subtle hazard: a test that forgets to call link_topic for a new artifact sees global decay behavior with no warning, which could mask the very scoping bug the new test in step 12 is meant to catch. the decay scope test does call link_topic for both topics, so it's safe; future authors may not. worth recording but not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-3-join-cutover"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-036",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: flag-017 (decay handler backward-compat fallback) is downgraded to accepted maintainability debt with an inline warning comment per step 4.5. the warning text explicitly tells future authors to call link_topic. acceptable, but a noisy assertion (e.g. log a warning when topic_id present in sql but row has no link) would catch silent regressions more loudly. not blocking.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-3-join-cutover"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: routing unit tests require extending fakepool with `channels`, `user_identities`, `bot_bindings`, `dyads`, and `dyad_members` dicts so the sql patterns in `resolve_bot`, `resolve_sender`, and `resolve_binding` can match against stored rows. however, `tests/conftest.py` is explicitly listed in the brief as a dirty file that must not be touched. the revised plan says \"extend fakepool minimally\" twice (step 9 and step 11 assumption) but never specifies how to do so without modifying `conftest.py`\u2014for example by defining a local `routingfakepool(fakepool)` subclass inside `tests/test_routing.py` or by monkeypatching the class in that test module. if the executor follows the plan literally and edits `conftest.py`, they will violate a locked constraint. if they do not extend fakepool, the routing unit tests cannot be implemented as written. this is a supporting-infrastructure gap.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py is still listed as a deliverable (step 2.1) and a smoke test (step 2.7 `--help` invocation). if t2 is deferred, the script could either (a) not be written, or (b) be written but its run deferred. plan should specify which. the smoke test alone (`--help`) does not require the pooler.",

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-003",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: new issue: the 0020 migration ddl defines `channels` with `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)`. postgresql treats null != null under standard unique constraints, so two whatsapp rows with `guild_id=null, channel_id=null` and the same transport+address would not conflict. however, `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, which requires a matching unique index on those coalesce expressions. without it, the seed script will raise 'there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification'. the ddl should either use `unique nulls not distinct` (pg15+, and the repo uses pg16 per `test_eval_workflow.py:36`) or a unique index on the coalesce expressions.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-017",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: step 13.2 adds `bot_id` kwarg to hooks.paused_for_user. callers grep shows it is called from various places; the plan only enumerates messaging.py:170,335 as new bot_id-passing sites. other callers continue to call without bot_id (none default), which is fine, but plan should call out that all existing callers continue to work without modification.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-025",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: evals.runner.run_scenarios is referenced by the deferred step 2.1; plan doesn't enumerate that runner's callers or its schema dependencies. since step 2 is being deferred, this is moot \u2014 but if the script is still authored as a step 2.1 deliverable, it must not import anything that hits the pooler at import time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: plan step 18 strips `bot_spec` defaults from `tests/_scope_helpers.py:make_mediator_ctx`, which has six call sites across `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` (5 calls) and `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` (1 call) that rely on the implicit default. the executor must update all six call sites to pass `bot_spec` explicitly. however, plan step 20's stop-condition nuance lists only steps 14\u201317 as the 'intentional-update list' and omits step 18. if the executor misses a call site and those tests fail, the stop condition text would classify the failure as 'unexpected' and trigger stop, even though the root cause is an incomplete step 18 execution. the stop condition should explicitly include step 18 (or enumerate `test_s2a_stamping.py` and `test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py`) so the executor has permission to fix residual failures in those files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "DEBT-005",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the architecture doc \u00a712.2 ddl for `user_identities` includes a `verified_at timestamptz` column. the plan's 0020 migration ddl omits this column entirely. while account-linking is deferred to s4+, omitting the column now means a future `alter table user_identities add column verified_at` is required, creating unnecessary schema churn. the column can safely be created nullable with no data in s1, matching the architecture doc verbatim.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-006",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the architecture doc \u00a712.1 ddl for `channels` includes `config jsonb not null default '{}'::jsonb`. the plan's 0020 migration ddl omits this column. like `verified_at`, this is a small additive column that should be created now to match the locked schema; deferring it requires a later migration. the executor should add the column to the 0020 ddl.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: step 10 claims `populate_mediator_spec_from_db(pool)` reads 'db-populated fields' for `primary_topic_slug`, `participants_shape`, `read_scopes`, etc. however, the `bots` table ddl in the plan only defines `id`, `display_name`, and `created_at` \u2014 none of the botspec scope fields have db columns. in practice the function will hardcode mediator defaults (`primary_topic_slug='relationship'`, `participants_shape='dyad'`) and only read `display_name` from the db row. the plan's wording could mislead the executor into writing a `select * from bots` expecting columns that do not exist. the implementation should explicitly select only `display_name` and hardcode the rest, or the ddl should be expanded to store bot config. the architecture doc does not specify these columns on `bots`, so hardcoding is correct for s1, but the plan text should be clarified.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the locked brief \u00a716.1 work item 1 explicitly requires 'channel rows for current discord + whatsapp' seeded inside migration 0020. the revised plan instead moves channel seeding to a runtime script `scripts/seed_channels.py`. while the rationale (env vars differ across environments) is pragmatic, this is a concrete divergence from the locked architecture-doc brief which the plan must consume, not override. if the executor applies only the sql migrations, the `channels` table will be empty and routing will have no rows to resolve, breaking the mid-sprint checkpoint 'channel rows for current discord + whatsapp' gate. the runtime script must be documented as a mandatory post-migration step, but the brief expected it in the migration itself.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]
    },
    {

      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",

      {
        "id": "DEBT-001",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },

        "id": "DEBT-004",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the 0020 migration plan for user_identities omits the verified_at timestamptz column present in architecture doc \u00a712.2 ddl, while claiming 'exactly per \u00a73/\u00a712 ddl'. since account-linking workflows are deferred beyond s1, the column is not functionally load-bearing yet, but the claim of exact ddl match is incorrect.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [

        ]

        "id": "DEBT-009",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the resolve_binding sql in app/services/routing.py uses limit 1 without order by. if a user ever has both a solo binding and a dyad binding to the same bot, the returned row is non-deterministic. the schema has no unique constraint preventing multiple bindings per (bot_id, user_id). since s1 only seeds one dyad binding and the module is not wired into inbound yet, this is latent future risk rather than an immediate s1 blocker.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
